Statements of Mathlib (commit a88727a581), each with its Lean source, its translation to Dedukti by Export.lean, and its informalization by Informath, in English, as a formula, and in French, Czech, German and Swedish, which the buttons at each entry show there, and the check boxes above the entries everywhere. The Dedukti translation drops implicit and instance arguments and maps Lean's arithmetic and logic to Informath's base (mapping.tsv); the other Mathlib constants are worded by the symbol table mathlib.dkgf, whose English wordings Informath also renders in the other languages. Constants without a wording appear as $\mathrm{name}$.

Nat.xgcddefGCD.lean:62
Use the extended GCD algorithm to generate the a and b values satisfying gcd x y = x * a + y * b.
Lean
def xgcd (x y : ℕ) : ℤ × ℤ :=
  (xgcdAux x 1 0 y 0 1).2
Dedukti
2 lines
def Nat__xgcd : (x : Elem Nat) -> (y : Elem Nat) -> Elem (Prod Int Int) :=
  x => y => Prod__snd (Nat__xgcdAux x 1 0 y 0 1).
English
Let $x , y \in \Natural$. Then $\operatorname{xgcd}$ applied to $x$ and $y$ is an element of $\ProdMACRoCN {\Integer}{\Integer}$ defined as $\ProdsndMACRoBL {\operatorname{xgcdAux} (x , 1 , 0 , y , 0 , 1)}$.
Formula
$x , y \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{xgcd} (x , y):= \ProdsndMACRoBS {\operatorname{xgcdAux} (x , 1 , 0 , y , 0 , 1)}\in \ProdMACRoCW {\Integer}{\Integer}$

Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_abtheoremGCD.lean:124
*Bézout's lemma: given x y : ℕ, gcd x y = x a + y * b, where a = gcd_a x y and b = gcd_b x y are computed by the extended Euclidean algorithm.
Lean
theorem gcd_eq_gcd_ab : (gcd x y : ℤ) = x * gcdA x y + y * gcdB x y := by
  have := @xgcdAux_P x y x y 1 0 0 1 (by simp [P]) (by simp [P])
  rwa [xgcdAux_val, xgcd_val] at this
Dedukti
1 lines
Nat__gcd_eq_gcd_ab : (x : Elem Nat) -> (y : Elem Nat) -> Proof (Eq (gcd x y) (plus (times x (Nat__gcdA x y)) (times y (Nat__gcdB x y)))).
English
Let $x , y \in \Natural$. Then the greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$ is equal to the sum of the product of $x$ and the greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$ and the product of $y$ and the greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$.

4.The greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$ is equal to the sum of the product of $x$ and the greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$ and the product of $y$ and the greatest common divisor of $x$ and $y$.rwa [xgcdAux_val, xgcd_val] at this
Nat.exists_mul_mod_eq_gcdtheoremGCD.lean:130
Lean
theorem exists_mul_mod_eq_gcd {k n : ℕ} (hk : gcd n k < k) : ∃ m < k, n * m % k = gcd n k := by
  have hk' := Int.ofNat_ne_zero.2 (Nat.zero_lt_of_lt hk).ne'
  have key := congr(($(gcd_eq_gcd_ab n k) % k).toNat)
  rw [Int.add_mul_emod_self_left, ← Int.natCast_mod, Int.toNat_natCast, mod_eq_of_lt hk] at key

theorem gcd_eq_gcd_ab : ∀ x y : ℤ, (gcd x y : ℤ) = x * gcdA x y + y * gcdB x y
  | (m : ℕ), (n : ℕ) => Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ab _ _
  | (m : ℕ), -[n+1] =>
    show (_ : ℤ) = _ + -(n + 1) * -_ by rw [Int.neg_mul_neg]; apply Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ab
  | -[m+1], (n : ℕ) =>
    show (_ : ℤ) = -(m + 1) * -_ + _ by rw [Int.neg_mul_neg]; apply Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ab
  | -[m+1], -[n+1] =>
    show (_ : ℤ) = -(m + 1) * -_ + -(n + 1) * -_ by
      rw [Int.neg_mul_neg, Int.neg_mul_neg]
      apply Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ab

      (dvd_mul_of_dvd_left (gcd_dvd_right ..))

ZMod.valdefBasic.lean:57
val a is a natural number defined as: - for a : ZMod 0 it is the absolute value of a - for a : ZMod n with 0 < n it is the least natural number in the equivalence class
See ZMod.valMinAbs for a variant that takes values in the integers.

theorem cast_add_eq_ite {n : ℕ} (a b : ZMod n) :
    (cast (a + b) : ℤ) =
      if (n : ℤ) ≤ cast a + cast b then (cast a + cast b - n : ℤ) else cast a + cast b := by
  rcases n with - | n
  · simp; rfl
  change Fin (n + 1) at a b
  change ((((a + b) : Fin (n + 1)) : ℕ) : ℤ) = if ((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℤ) ≤ (a : ℕ) + b then _ else _
  simp only [Fin.val_add_eq_ite, Int.natCast_succ]
  norm_cast
  split_ifs with h
  · rw [Nat.cast_sub h]
    congr
  · rfl

@[simp]
theorem cast_one (h : m ∣ n) : (cast (1 : ZMod n) : R) = 1 := by
  rcases n with - | n
  · exact Int.cast_one
  change ((1 % (n + 1) : ℕ) : R) = 1
  cases n
  · rw [Nat.dvd_one] at h
    subst m
    subsingleton [CharP.CharOne.subsingleton]
  rw [Nat.mod_eq_of_lt]
  · exact Nat.cast_one
  exact Nat.lt_of_sub_eq_succ rfl

1 lines
ZMod__cast_one : (n : Elem Nat) -> (R : Set) -> (_ : Proof (Ring R)) -> (m : Elem Nat) -> (_ : Proof (CharP R m)) -> (h1 : Proof (divide m n)) -> Proof (Eqt R (ZMod__cast 1) 1).
English
Let $n , m \in \Natural$. Let $R$ be a ring. Assume that $m$ is characteristic. Assume that $m$ divides $n$. Then the cast of $1$ is equal to $1$.
Formula
$n \in \Natural , R \in \mathrm{Set} , \mathrm{Ring} (R), m \in \Natural , \mathrm{CharP} (R , m), \divides {m}{n}\vdash \EqtMACRoAU {\operatorname{cast} (1)}{1}$
Proof
13 lines
1.We reason by cases:rcases n with - | n
2.Assume that $\divides {m}{0}$.
3.The cast of $1$ is equal to $1$.exact Int.cast_one
4.Assume that $\divides {m}{n + 1}$.
5.It is enough to show that $\EqtMACRoAU {\HModhModMACRoAS {1}{(n + 1)}}{1}$.change ((1 % (n + 1) : ℕ) : R) = 1
6.We reason by cases:cases n
7.Assume that $\divides {m}{0 + 1}$.
8.$m = 1$.rw [Nat.dvd_one] at h
9.$\EqtMACRoAU {\HModhModMACRoAS {1}{(0 + 1)}}{1}$.subsingleton [CharP.CharOne.subsingleton]
10.Assume that $\divides {m}{\mathrm{n'} + 1 + 1}$.
11.It is enough to show that $\EqtMACRoAU {1}{1}$ and $1 < \mathrm{n'} + 1 + 1$.rw [Nat.mod_eq_of_lt]
12.$\EqtMACRoAU {1}{1}$.exact Nat.cast_one
13.$1 < \mathrm{n'} + 1 + 1$.exact Nat.lt_of_sub_eq_succ rfl
ZMod.cast_addtheoremBasic.lean:307
Lean
@[simp]
theorem cast_add (h : m ∣ n) (a b : ZMod n) : (cast (a + b : ZMod n) : R) = cast a + cast b := by
  cases n
  · apply Int.cast_add
  symm
  dsimp [ZMod, ZMod.cast, ZMod.val]
  rw [← Nat.cast_add, Fin.val_add, ← sub_eq_zero, ← Nat.cast_sub (Nat.mod_le _ _),
    @CharP.cast_eq_zero_iff R _ m]
  exact h.trans (Nat.dvd_sub_mod _)

ZMod.castHomdefBasic.lean:331
The canonical ring homomorphism from ZMod n to a ring of characteristic dividing n.
See also ZMod.lift for a generalized version working in AddGroups.
Lean
def castHom (h : m ∣ n) (R : Type*) [Ring R] [CharP R m] : ZMod n →+* R where
  toFun := cast
  map_zero' := cast_zero
  map_one' := cast_one h
  map_add' := cast_add h
  map_mul' := cast_mul h
Dedukti
2 lines
def ZMod__castHom : (h : Proof (divide m n)) -> (R : Set) -> Elem (RingHom (ZMod n) R) :=
  h => R => RingHom__mk (MonoidHom__mk (OneHom__mk ZMod__cast (ZMod__cast_one h)) (ZMod__cast_mul h)) (ZMod__castHom___proof_1 R) (ZMod__cast_add h).
English
Assume that $m$ divides $n$. Let $R$ be a set. Then the cast homomorphism of $h$ and $R$ is a ring homomorphism from $\ZModMACRoEP {n}$ to $R$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{cast}$ and $\mathrm{ZMod\_\_cast\_one}$ applied to $h$ and $\mathrm{ZMod\_\_cast\_mul}$ applied to $h$, $\mathrm{ZMod\_\_castHom\_\_\_proof\_1}$ applied to $R$ and $\mathrm{ZMod\_\_cast\_add}$ applied to $h$.
Formula

theorem castHom_bijective [Fintype R] (h : Fintype.card R = n) :
    Function.Bijective (ZMod.castHom (dvd_refl n) R) := by
  have : NeZero n :=

      intro hn

  apply ZMod.castHom_injective

theorem cast_injective_of_le {m n : ℕ} [nzm : NeZero m] (h : m ≤ n) :
    Function.Injective (@cast (ZMod n) _ m) := by
  cases m with
  | zero => cases nzm; simp_all
  | succ m =>
    rintro ⟨x, hx⟩ ⟨y, hy⟩ f
    simp only [cast, val, natCast_eq_natCast_iff',
      Nat.mod_eq_of_lt (hx.trans_le h), Nat.mod_eq_of_lt (hy.trans_le h)] at f
    apply Fin.ext
    exact f

ZMod.invdefBasic.lean:716
The inversion on ZMod n. It is setup in such a way that a * a⁻¹ is equal to gcd a.val n. In particular, if a is coprime to n, and hence a unit, a * a⁻¹ = 1.

ZMod.mul_inv_eq_gcdtheoremBasic.lean:729
Lean
theorem mul_inv_eq_gcd {n : ℕ} (a : ZMod n) : a * a⁻¹ = Nat.gcd a.val n := by
  rcases n with - | n
  · dsimp [ZMod] at a ⊢
    calc
      _ = a * Int.sign a := rfl
      _ = a.natAbs := by rw [Int.mul_sign_self]
      _ = a.natAbs.gcd 0 := by rw [Nat.gcd_zero_right]
  · calc
      a * a⁻¹ = a * a⁻¹ + n.succ * Nat.gcdB (val a) n.succ := by
        rw [natCast_self, zero_mul, add_zero]
      _ = ↑(↑a.val * Nat.gcdA (val a) n.succ + n.succ * Nat.gcdB (val a) n.succ) := by
        push_cast
        rw [natCast_zmod_val]
        rfl
      _ = Nat.gcd a.val n.succ := by rw [← Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ab a.val n.succ]; rfl

  exact Nat.gcd_rec n m

ZMod.chineseRemainderdefBasic.lean:889
The *Chinese remainder theorem. For a pair of coprime natural numbers, m and n, the rings ZMod (m n) and ZMod m × ZMod n are isomorphic.
See Ideal.quotientInfRingEquivPiQuotient for the Chinese remainder theorem for ideals in any ring.
Lean
def chineseRemainder {m n : ℕ} (h : m.Coprime n) : ZMod (m * n) ≃+* ZMod m × ZMod n :=
  let to_fun : ZMod (m * n) → ZMod m × ZMod n :=
    ZMod.castHom (show m.lcm n ∣ m * n by simp [Nat.lcm_dvd_iff]) (ZMod m × ZMod n)
  let inv_fun : ZMod m × ZMod n → ZMod (m * n) := fun x =>
    if m * n = 0 then
      if m = 1 then cast (RingHom.snd _ (ZMod n) x) else cast (RingHom.fst (ZMod m) _ x)
    else Nat.chineseRemainder h x.1.val x.2.val
  have inv : Function.LeftInverse inv_fun to_fun ∧ Function.RightInverse inv_fun to_fun :=
    if hmn0 : m * n = 0 then by
      rcases h.eq_of_mul_eq_zero hmn0 with (⟨rfl, rfl⟩ | ⟨rfl, rfl⟩)
      · constructor
        · intro x; rfl
        · rintro ⟨x, y⟩
          fin_cases y
          simp [to_fun, inv_fun, castHom, Prod.ext_iff, eq_iff_true_of_subsingleton]
      · constructor
        · intro x; rfl
        · rintro ⟨x, y⟩
          fin_cases x
          simp [to_fun, inv_fun, castHom, Prod.ext_iff, eq_iff_true_of_subsingleton]
    else by
      have : NeZero (m * n) := ⟨hmn0⟩
      have : NeZero m := ⟨left_ne_zero_of_mul hmn0⟩
      have : NeZero n := ⟨right_ne_zero_of_mul hmn0⟩
      have left_inv : Function.LeftInverse inv_fun to_fun := by
        intro x
        dsimp only [to_fun, inv_fun, ZMod.castHom_apply]
        conv_rhs => rw [← ZMod.natCast_zmod_val x]
        rw [ite_eq_right hmn0, ZMod.natCast_eq_natCast_iff, ← Nat.modEq_and_modEq_iff_modEq_mul h,
          Prod.fst_zmod_cast, Prod.snd_zmod_cast]
        refine
          ⟨(Nat.chineseRemainder h (cast x : ZMod m).val (cast x : ZMod n).val).2.left.trans ?_,
            (Nat.chineseRemainder h (cast x : ZMod m).val (cast x : ZMod n).val).2.right.trans ?_⟩
        · rw [← ZMod.natCast_eq_natCast_iff, ZMod.natCast_zmod_val, ZMod.natCast_val]
        · rw [← ZMod.natCast_eq_natCast_iff, ZMod.natCast_zmod_val, ZMod.natCast_val]
      exact ⟨left_inv, left_inv.rightInverse_of_card_le (by simp)⟩
  { toFun := to_fun,
    invFun := inv_fun,
    map_mul' := map_mul _
    map_add' := map_add _
    left_inv := inv.1
    right_inv := inv.2 }

lemma subsingleton_iff {n : ℕ} : Subsingleton (ZMod n) ↔ n = 1 := by
  constructor
  · obtain (_ | _ | n) := n
    · simpa [ZMod] using not_subsingleton _
    · simp [ZMod]
    · simpa [ZMod] using not_subsingleton _
  · rintro rfl
    infer_instance

ZMod__subsingleton_iff : (n : Elem Nat) -> Proof (iff (Subsingleton (ZMod n)) (Eq n 1)).
English
Let $n \in \Natural$. Then $\ZModMACRoEP {n}$ is a subsingleton, if and only if $n = 1$.
Formula
$n \in \Natural \vdash \mathrm{Subsingleton} (\ZModMACRoFB {n})\leftrightarrow n = 1$
Proof
7 lines
1.It is enough to show that if $\ZModMACRoFB {n}$ is a subsingleton, then $n = 1$ and if $n = 1$, then $\ZModMACRoFB {n}$ is a subsingleton.constructor
2.We reason by cases:obtain (_ | _ | n) := n
3.If $\ZModMACRoFB {0}$ is a subsingleton, then $0 = 1$.simpa [ZMod] using not_subsingleton _
4.If $\ZModMACRoFB {(0 + 1)}$ is a subsingleton, then $0 + 1 = 1$.simp [ZMod]
5.If $\ZModMACRoFB {(n + 1 + 1)}$ is a subsingleton, then $n + 1 + 1 = 1$.simpa [ZMod] using not_subsingleton _
6.To prove that $\ZModMACRoFB {n}$ is a subsingleton, assume that $n = 1$.rintro rfl
7.$\ZModMACRoFB {1}$ is a subsingleton.infer_instance

theorem neg_eq_self_iff {n : ℕ} (a : ZMod n) : -a = a ↔ a = 0 ∨ 2 * a.val = n := by

      rcases he with (⟨⟨⟩⟩ | he)
      exact Or.inl (a.val_eq_zero.1 he)
    cases m
    · right
      rwa [show 0 + 1 = 1 from rfl, mul_one] at he
    refine (a.val_lt.not_ge <| Nat.le_of_mul_le_mul_left ?_ zero_lt_two).elim

    apply Nat.mul_le_mul_left
    simp

    · rw [val_zero, mul_zero]
      apply dvd_zero
    · rw [h]

theorem val_pow {m n : ℕ} {a : ZMod n} [ilt : Fact (1 < n)] (h : a.val ^ m < n) :
    (a ^ m).val = a.val ^ m := by
  induction m with
  | zero => simp [ZMod.val_one]
  | succ m ih =>
    have : a.val ^ m < n := by
      obtain rfl | ha := eq_or_ne a 0
      · by_cases hm : m = 0
        · cases hm; simp [ilt.out]
        · simp only [val_zero, ne_eq, hm, not_false_eq_true, zero_pow, Nat.zero_lt_of_lt h]
      · exact lt_of_le_of_lt
         (Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by rwa [gt_iff_lt, ZMod.val_pos]) (Nat.le_succ m)) h
    rw [pow_succ, ZMod.val_mul, ih this, ← pow_succ, Nat.mod_eq_of_lt h]

theorem natAbs_min_of_le_div_two (n : ℕ) (x y : ℤ) (he : (x : ZMod n) = y) (hl : x.natAbs ≤ n / 2) :
    x.natAbs ≤ y.natAbs := by

  obtain ⟨m, he⟩ := he

  refine le_trans ?_ (Nat.le_mul_of_pos_right _ <| Int.natAbs_pos.2 hm)
  rw [← mul_two]; apply Nat.div_mul_le_self

theorem RingHom.ext_zmod {n : ℕ} {R : Type*} [NonAssocSemiring R] (f g : ZMod n →+* R) : f = g := by
  ext a
  obtain ⟨k, rfl⟩ := ZMod.intCast_surjective a
  let φ : ℤ →+* R := f.comp (Int.castRingHom (ZMod n))
  let ψ : ℤ →+* R := g.comp (Int.castRingHom (ZMod n))
  change φ k = ψ k
  rw [φ.ext_int ψ]

The range of (m * · + k) on natural numbers is the set of elements ≥ k in the residue class of k mod m.

lemma Nat.range_mul_add (m k : ℕ) :
    Set.range (fun n : ℕ ↦ m * n + k) = {n : ℕ | (n : ZMod m) = k ∧ k ≤ n} := by
  ext n
  simp only [Set.mem_range, Set.mem_ofPred_eq]
  conv => enter [1, 1, y]; rw [add_comm, eq_comm]
  refine ⟨fun ⟨a, ha⟩ ↦ ⟨?_, le_iff_exists_add.mpr ⟨_, ha⟩⟩, fun ⟨H₁, H₂⟩ ↦ ?_⟩
  · simpa using congr(($ha : ZMod m))
  · obtain ⟨a, ha⟩ := le_iff_exists_add.mp H₂
    simp only [ha, Nat.cast_add, add_eq_left, ZMod.natCast_eq_zero_iff] at H₁
    obtain ⟨b, rfl⟩ := H₁
    exact ⟨b, ha⟩

def Nat.residueClassesEquiv (N : ℕ) [NeZero N] : ℕ ≃ ZMod N × ℕ where
  toFun n := (↑n, n / N)
  invFun p := p.1.val + N * p.2
  left_inv n := by simpa only [val_natCast] using mod_add_div n N
  right_inv p := by
    ext1
    · simp only [add_comm p.1.val, cast_add, cast_mul, natCast_self, zero_mul, natCast_val,
        cast_id', id_eq, zero_add]
    · simp only [add_comm p.1.val, mul_add_div (NeZero.pos _),
        (Nat.div_eq_zero_iff).2 <| .inr p.1.val_lt, add_zero]

Rat__den_eq_one_iff : (r : Elem Rat) -> Proof (iff (Eq (Rat__den r) 1) (Eq (Rat__num r) r)).
English
Let $r \in \Rational$. Then the denominator of $r$ is equal to $1$, if and only if the numerator of $r$ is equal to $r$.

Rat__divCasesOn : (a : Elem Rat) -> (div : ((n : Elem Int) -> (d : Elem Nat) -> Proof (Neq d 0) -> Proof (Nat__Coprime (Int__natAbs n) d) -> C (div n d))) -> Elem (C a).
English
Let $a \in \Rational$. Let $\mathrm{div}$ be a function from integers $n$ and natural numbers $d$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\mathrm{Neq} (d , 0)$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\NatCoprimeMACRoAN {\IntnatAbsMACRoBJ {n}}{d}$ to elements of $C$ applied to $\frac{ n}{d}$. Then $\operatorname{divCasesOn}$ applied to $a$, $n$, $d$ and $\mathrm{div}$ is an element of $C$ applied to $a$.
Formula
$a \in \Rational , \mathrm{div} \in \Integer \rightarrow \Natural \rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{Neq} (d , 0))\rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\NatCoprimeMACRoAO {\IntnatAbsMACRoBQ {n}}{d})\rightarrow C (\frac{ n}{d})\vdash \operatorname{divCasesOn} (a , n , d , \mathrm{div})\in C (a)$
